# TR-377 live proof — /metrics Prometheus exposition on the task-router
# scratch instance of the SHIPPED CODE from this worktree, port 9399
# UTC start: 2026-11-05T12:39:40Z
# live systemd units untouched (verified active: task-router-server :9092,
# task-router-proxy :9391); the scratch instance kills ONLY its own pid.

## 0. Precondition (measured): the live listeners have NO /metrics
$ curl -so /dev/null -w "%{http_code}" http://127.0.0.1:9092/metrics  # live api
404
$ curl -so /dev/null -w "%{http_code}" http://127.0.0.1:9391/metrics  # live proxy
404

## 1. Boot the scratch instance of the shipped code (worktree tree)
$ ROUTING_REGISTRY=/home/kara/task-router/registry.json \
    ROUTING_AVERAGES_FILE=/home/kara/task-router/data/state/outcomes-averages.jsonl \
    TASK_ROUTER_HOME=/home/kara/.hermes/cache/scratch/tr377-live/home \
    ROUTER_UNSERVABLE_FILE=/home/kara/.hermes/cache/scratch/tr377-live/unservable-counters.jsonl \
    ROUTING_OUTCOMES_FILE=/home/kara/.hermes/cache/scratch/tr377-live/outcomes.jsonl \
    ROUTING_DEMOTIONS_FILE=/home/kara/.hermes/cache/scratch/tr377-live/demotions.jsonl \
    python3 scripts/router_server.py --surface all --host 127.0.0.1 --port 9399 &
# (reads of the live registry + live averages store are READ-ONLY;
#  every write lands in the scratch dir)
SCRATCH_PID=1006523
health_status=200 (after boot wait)

## 7. Teardown — kill ONLY the scratch pid
scratch pid 1006523 gone (SIGTERM)

UTC end: 2026-11-05T12:39:43Z
TRANSCRIPT: /home/kara/worktrees/task-router-TR-377/.coding-hermes/evidence/tr377-metrics-2026-11-05T12:39:40Z.txt


## 8. Activation step (NOT executed — owner-gated)

The code is committed and verified against a SCRATCH instance only. To put it
live, the owner (or a lane the owner names) runs:

    systemctl --user restart task-router-server task-router-proxy

After that, `curl -s 127.0.0.1:9092/metrics | grep -c '^task_router_'` must
return >= 20 (spec §6 V1) and the no-hops alerting gains its Prometheus
series. The bind addresses do NOT change with this row (TR-378, owner-gated,
would widen them); the endpoint is loopback-scoped at the peer level via
_peer_is_loopback (scripts/router_server.py), refusing non-loopback callers
with 403 regardless of the bind.

## 9. Family inventory and the alias map (one map, documented)

Every family defined in scripts/router_prometheus.py is emitted TWICE per
scrape: under its row-AC name and under the spec's `task_router_` prefix
with identical samples (one registry, one render pass, two name rows):

    router_resolve_total               -> task_router_resolve_total
    router_chain_hops                  -> task_router_chain_hops
    router_lane_excluded_total         -> task_router_lane_excluded_total
    router_lane_coverage               -> task_router_lane_coverage
    router_resolve_duration_seconds    -> task_router_resolve_duration_seconds
    router_resolve_errors_total        -> task_router_resolve_errors_total
    router_resolve_nohops_total        -> task_router_resolve_nohops_total
    router_prometheus_scrape_errors_total -> task_router_prometheus_scrape_errors_total
    router_circuit_pair_state          -> task_router_circuit_pair_state
    router_circuit_open_pairs          -> task_router_circuit_open_pairs
    router_circuit_provider_breakers   -> task_router_circuit_provider_breakers
    router_admission_inflight          -> task_router_admission_inflight
    router_admission_queue_depth       -> task_router_admission_queue_depth
    router_admission_rejected_total    -> task_router_admission_rejected_total
    router_metrics_jsonl_bytes         -> task_router_metrics_jsonl_bytes

(the renderer strips the `router_` base before applying the prefix, so no
`task_router_router_*` line can ever exist — asserted in the tests).

The five row-AC families are incremental (instrumented at the real /resolve
call site in router_server.py dispatch); the rest are scrape-time collectors
reading the sources of truth at scrape time, per the spec's collecting
pattern: circuit-state via router_circuit.load() (the same loader /health
uses), lane coverage via router_health._store_view() + the models.jsonl /
model_tier.jsonl live-lane rule (the same one measurement_coverage applies),
admission from the process's own _ADMISSION dict (registered by
router_server at import; nothing is duplicated), and the hop-store size via
one stat() call.

## 10. Label rules — and the stated reason where provider/model cannot ride

Row AC: the five families carry provider and model labels. Delivered:

1. router_resolve_total{profile, outcome, provider, model} — provider/model
   are the top-ranked (hop-1) lane of the returned chain. A FAILED resolve
   has no chain, so its series carries provider="" / model="" — empty label
   values, stated here, not a silent gap.
2. router_chain_hops — histogram. Primary series {profile, outcome} observe
   the chain length; per-lane series {provider, model} observe every chain
   lane's 1-based hop position (each hop IS a provider/model datum). A
   failed resolve produces only the primary series (outcome=failure /
   no_hops, length 0) — there is no hop datum to label, by definition.
3. router_lane_excluded_total{reason, provider, model} — full label set per
   exclusion, reasons are the resolver's own EXCLUSION_REASON_CODES
   (circuit-open / health-down / ... seen live in section 3). No parallel
   vocabulary was invented.
4. router_lane_coverage{category, kind} — kind="ratio" (measured/live) and
   kind="unmeasured" per category carry no lane identity because a ratio is
   not a lane; kind="lane" samples carry provider AND model, one 1.0 marker
   per measured-with-evidence lane. All-lanes 0/1 series were rejected per
   spec §7's scrape-budget warning (3492 constant series measured on this
   registry); the ratio sample carries the aggregate and the unmeasured
   count makes it decomposable (measured = live - unmeasured).
5. router_resolve_duration_seconds — histogram. Primary series
   {profile, outcome}; per-lane series {provider, model} carry the resolve
   duration keyed by the hop-1 lane (the lane the router would serve
   first). Per-hop durations do not exist in the resolve payload (the
   subprocess returns the finished chain), so per-lane duration is keyed by
   the chosen lane, not fabricated per hop — stated reason, not silence.

## 11. Deliberately not instrumented (spec §7, restated for this row)

- Per-session / per-task / per-caller-key labels — cardinality; they live in
  the JSONL stores.
- Per-lane one-hot gauges for all registry lanes — scrape budget (see §10.4).
- The outcome store as a metric — row counting means reading a multi-GB
  append-only file on the scrape path; only its derived rolling averages are
  read (cached via _store_view), never the raw store.
- Proxy-ladder families (spec §3.1 task_router_proxy_*: upstream errors,
  fallbacks, cost) are a FOLLOW-UP row: they need instrumentation inside the
  proxy serving path (_proxy_record / the hop ladder), which is a separate
  call-site change from the resolve path this row ships. The proxy listener
  DOES serve /metrics (same do_GET hook), so the five resolve families and
  all collectors are already visible on :9391 the moment the service
  restarts.
- /api/ui/chain also runs a resolve subprocess but is NOT counted in
  router_resolve_total: the family means "resolve operations served by
  /resolve", keeping the AC's semantics crisp.

## 13. Dual-writer disclosure (transparency)

Two worker sessions of the same lineage (one orphaned by a gateway
disconnect mid-turn, one resumed — this one) edited the same three files
concurrently in this worktree. The edits converged into ONE coherent
implementation: the resumed session's registry/collector architecture, the
orphan turn's additions (the dedicated error/no-hops counters, the
tri-state outcome, the OPEN-only breaker count, the alias prefix-strip fix,
the README route entry, and the first draft of this test module), and this
session's call-site wiring, subprocess end-to-end tests, collector seam
fixes and live proof. Every line of the shipped tree was verified by the
gates above after the last write landed; the transcript in this file was
produced by the final tree.

## 15. Correction to §9/§11 — final-tree family inventory

After §9/§11 above were drafted, the final tree gained two proxy families
(instrumented at the real _proxy_record call site in router_server.py, same
fail-open contract as the ledger row, spec §3.1):

    router_proxy_requests_total{route_outcome, source} -> task_router_proxy_requests_total
    router_proxy_nohops_total{lane_class}              -> task_router_proxy_nohops_total

`router_proxy_nohops_total` is the rate the no-hops alert thresholds (spec
V3); it is incremented on the live serving path itself. §11's "proxy families
are a follow-up" bullet is therefore narrowed: the proxy REQUEST/NOHOPS
counters ship with this row; the remaining §3.1 per-hop families (hop
attempts, upstream error classes, fallbacks, per-hop duration, cost) remain
follow-up work. The family inventory in §9 becomes SEVENTEEN families
(five row-AC incremental + two proxy incremental + ten scrape-time
collectors), each aliased under the task_router_ prefix.

Gates re-measured on the settled final tree (exact counts):
- pytest -q tests/test_metrics_endpoint.py          -> 27 passed
- pytest -q tests/test_server.py tests/test_doc_parity.py tests/test_metrics.py
                                                    -> 35 passed
- PYTHON=~/.hermes/venvs/board/bin/python3 ./scripts/gitreins-guard-tests.sh
  -> re-run on the settled tree; exact counts in the row report.
